# Port note: UI-12 (the layout grid)

Package UI-12 of the UI refresh ([docs/specs/ui-refresh-plan.md](../specs/ui-refresh-plan.md), section 7's UI-12 row). The owner's decision of 2026-11-21
replaces "restyle only, layouts unchanged": **every window is laid out again on one spacing grid by a tool** (same controls, same window sizes where they
fit), a pilot first (this package, branch `ui/ui12p-1`), then every window in slices; and **the check box's box is on the left of its text,
everywhere**. Why: the owner's review of the UI-10 text-fit galleries ("overall not good. UI lacking consistence. choice of spacing not symetrical"; the
38 rows of [UI-12-owner-review.tsv](UI-12-owner-review.tsv)). The owner's standard for the result: "apply general styling sense. Looking for symmetry,
consistency, right balance between too much crowding and too much spacing". The numbers below are the starting point, not the goal.

## 1. The grid

In pixels at 96 dpi (100%), for the program's font after UI-2b (Segoe UI 9 pt). The planner reads every number from one table,
`tests/UiRefreshTests/Data/grid-numbers.tsv` (`GRID` in `tools/UiFit/grid_tools.py`; `CodeBase/UI/UiGrid.cs` for product code, UI-12T),
so a change here is a change there.

- **Container padding** 8 px on every side (form client area, panel, tab page, group box). A group box's content starts 8 px below its title band;
  nothing covers a title.
- **Labels.** A label sits 6 px before its field, text right-aligned, vertically centred on the field. Labels of one column share their right edge;
  fields of one column share their left edge.
- **Single-line fields** (text box, combo box, date picker, number field: `ValidDouble`, `ValidNum`, up-down boxes and the like) use one standard
  height from the font. Rows have one pitch per container: the standard height + 4 px gap (the owner, round 2: fields 23 px, row pitch 27;
  round 1 had 6 px). Consecutive check boxes or radio buttons form a list with a compact pitch (box height + 4); rows that both start with a
  check box in one column are one list even when a field or note stands further along a row. A button in a field row has the field height.
- **Preferences style** (round 2, the lead's audit; every Preferences page and any group of mostly check boxes): a label starts where the
  check box text beside it starts (the box's x + 16 px), left-aligned; every field of the page shares **one left edge**, the same in every
  group (the widest label + 6 px, measured over the page in page coordinates); a label that would push that column past **50%** of the
  content width wraps onto two lines instead (the lead's decision of round 3, "not 60%": on the 494 px page the field column is at 256,
  rounds 2 and 3 had 304). Combo boxes, note boxes and fields 120 px or wider end at the content's right edge (one right
  edge for the page); narrow number boxes keep their width. A group box alone on its rows spans the page.
- **Shared field columns** (round 2): in ordinary forms the field columns of different containers (group boxes, tab pages, panels, the
  window) whose left edges are within 40 px in window coordinates share the rightmost one, so the fields of one column of the window start
  at one x across the group boxes stacked in it. Group boxes stacked in one column share one right edge. A check box in a group of label +
  field rows puts its box on the group's main field column.
- **Widths.** Fields of one column whose right edges are within about 16 px of each other share one right edge; other widths snap to the 4 px grid.
  Multi-line boxes, grids, lists and pictures keep their size, snapped to the grid, with their edges aligned with the column they belong to.
- **Check boxes.** `CheckAlign` MiddleLeft, `TextAlign` MiddleLeft, the box first. A check box in a column of label + field rows puts its box at the
  field column's left edge (its text after it); a check box in a check box list or a group of check boxes only puts its box at the content's left
  edge. AutoSize where the text fits in the row; otherwise the text wraps into a taller row. A control beside it (the "Sync" button) follows the text
  with 6 px.
- **Containers side by side** have 8 px between them. **Dialog buttons** (OK, Save, Cancel, Close) sit bottom right, 8 px apart, 8 px from the edges.
- **Growth.** A container grows (down first, then right) when its content needs it; the window grows only when it must, and never shrinks; the
  Preferences pages keep the width FormPreferences gives them and grow down.
- **Round 3** (the lead's audit of round 2 and the owner's addition "Drop down, radio, text also. Spacing between items should be the same.
  Text shouldn't be covered by other elements"): rows of one column that end in a field end at one right edge (the column's most common
  edge or its widest, within 16 px: City, ST, Zip under Address); lists side by side in a row get one top and one height, the captions over
  them start where they start, a row of buttons under them ends where the last list ends; the parts beside the lists of a column start at
  one x and the rightmost box of each row of a column ends at one edge (FormPractice's right column); a check box hidden in the designer on
  a row of shown controls and taller than they are goes to the end of its column; a one-line check box within 3 px of its row stays one
  line (GDI+ measures 2-3 px wider than the box draws); a tall list keeps the rows beside it in its row; the stock radio button is drawn
  like the check box (`UiRadioLook`: the circle where the box is, the text 16 px from the left), so radio buttons and check boxes share x
  and pitch.
- **Round 4** (the fix round, the lead's review of round 3): a **clinic picker** is a single-line field like every combo box: 23 px tall
  (the planner left it at the designer's 21), its own "Clinic" label drawn right-aligned 6 px before its box and centred on it, like the
  labels of the rows around it (it was drawn from x = 2 at a fixed top, touching the box); a **multi-line text box one line tall** (Referred
  From, FormScreenEdit's Birthdate) is a single-line field: 23 px, the row pitch and the shared single-line frame (`UiInputFrame`); a
  **wrapped note** is as tall as its lines and no taller (the larger of two estimates: a word wrap at the measured average character width,
  and the measured width over the room), so a group's bottom padding under a note is the 8 px of every group; a **row hidden in the
  designer** at the end of a group either stands beside a shown row where it fits (UserControlEnterpriseGeneral's lock note, never shown on
  that page) or the group ends 8 px under its last shown row and the code that shows the row grows the group by it and moves what is under
  it (FormPatientEdit's super family row, in Load; UserControlMainWindow's restricted clinics row, from FormPreferences after FillSynced);
  scaled (150%), the **program's combo box and the clinic picker** take the height of a single-line text box at the font text boxes get
  (LayoutManager), because the Windows inputs (text boxes, number fields, date pickers) take their height from their font and cannot be
  sized: one row of inputs has one height at every scale. **Code that sizes the window itself** (`Width`, `Height`, `Size`, `ClientSize`
  set on the form) is listed by the planner for every window (tool and hand), so a slice checks each size against the new layout; the
  pilot's two that broke are now derived from the designer (FormInsBenefits' simplified view width, FormPayment's hidden-splits height).
- **Round 5** (the second fix round, the lead's review of round 4): a **wider gap** between two rows (the section gap, 12 px) stays only
  where something explains it: a row hidden in the designer stood in it, or one of the two rows is a visible separator line (a panel or
  label 1-3 px tall); otherwise the rows take the gap of their kind (UserControlMainWindowMisc kept main's loose 12 px between two groups
  and between two fields). A **label that wraps beside its field** (the Preferences style) is as tall as its measured lines: a word wrap
  with the advance of each character as a label draws it (Segoe UI 9, measured with GDI+), scaled to the dump's measured one-line width;
  a line end after the text draws nothing (the width estimate made a two-line label three lines tall, 12 px off its field's centre, and
  missed the third line of "DateTime the currently running aging started / Usually blank"). **No line starts with a hyphen**: when the
  wrap would break a hyphenated word before its hyphen ("non" / "-primary"), the label narrows to halfway between the line without the
  word and the line with its first part, so the whole word moves down.
- **Round 6** (the polish round, the lead's non-blocking list of round 5): a **wide field** (120 px or wider) up to 32 px short of its
  column's right edge grows to it (`GRID["edge_grow"]`; narrower fields keep the 16 px of `edge_snap`, so number and code boxes keep their
  width): FormScreenEdit's Grade Level, FormProcCodeEdit's Description, FormProcEdit's Place of Service, FormProvEdit's Taxonomy Code
  Override. A **Preferences label beside a multi-line box** stands on the box's first line, as the labels of the ordinary forms do (it was
  centred on the box: UserControlTreatPlanGeneral's Default Note). **Every multi-line text box** with a border draws the single-line
  inputs' frame (`UiInputFrame`: the stock TextBox's square line, the RichTextBox's and ODtextBox's 3-D edge), and the **up-down boxes'
  arrow buttons** draw in the theme (`UiUpDownLook`: the box's fill, SubtleHover and SubtlePressed under the pointer, the combo box's Fluent
  chevrons in its token, Text). Both are shared-control restyles: no window's designer changes for them.
- **Plain windows** (slice 12): a `System.Windows.Forms.Form` that is not a FormODBase (the plain exes' windows: Central Manager, MySQL
  Manager, the integrity check, the documentation builder, the CRUD generator, the HL7 debug window; the graph's dashboard dialogs) and a
  UserControl only such a form hosts (the graph's controls) take the same numbers. They have no FormODBase frame: their tree's root is the
  form's client area (its bounds take Windows' frame and title bar; `grid_tools.plain_client_root`), no title band of their own, and no
  input frame (`UiInputFrame` is attached by FormODBase), so GridRulesTests checks them with every rule but the input frame one
  (`GridRulesTests.IsPlain`, test `PlainWindow_EveryRuleButTheInputFrame`). Windows draws their stock check boxes and radio buttons (no
  `UiRadioLook`): the text starts 22 px from the left (`check_width`'s plain lead; the stock check box's preferred width is the text + 22
  to 25), and they draw the box's TextAlign: a box turned box-left takes its text to the left too (MiddleRight -> MiddleLeft,
  `grid_tools.box_left_text_align`; with main's MiddleRight the text started 23 to 27 px after the box). Their System-style controls take
  the default Standard style (the classic drawing has no theme). At 150% the off-screen harness scales their bounds as Windows' DPI
  autoscale does (`BuiltWindow.LayOutAt`, test `PlainWindow_LayOutAt150_ScalesBounds`) and the gallery's font pass their fonts.
  UI-PLAIN ([UI-PLAIN.md](UI-PLAIN.md)) gives them the program's look since: each calls `UiPlainWindow.Attach` (the background token,
  the input frame and every shared look, moved to CodeBase/UI); their layout rules here are unchanged.
- **Untouched:** Dock and Anchor values, tab order, names, events, texts, docked controls, controls the code positions at run time (`Location`,
  `Bounds`, `Left`, `Top`, `Size` set in the window's `.cs`, or `LayoutManager.Move*`), controls stacked on the same bounds and switched by `Visible`
  at run time (they move together as one), and owner-drawn surfaces (the tooth chart, the appointment book, the sheet and eForm fill surfaces, claim
  forms, printouts).

### 1.1 The numbers, measured

From the control tree of the 47 pilot windows at Segoe UI 9 (`GridDump`, section 2):

| Number | Value | Measured / why |
|---|---|---|
| Single-line field height | **23 px** | `TextBox.PreferredHeight` of a bordered single-line text box at the program's font is 23 in every pilot window. The date picker and the up-down box take 23 from the font and cannot be made smaller, so 23 is the one height every input can share (the owner's FormPayment row asks for one look). The planner reads it from each window's tree (bordered text boxes only: a borderless box is 16). |
| Row pitch | **27 px** (23 + 4) | The owner's answer of 2026-11-21 (round 2); round 1 used 29 (23 + 6), which made long forms pass 1000 px. |
| Check box / radio row | **18 px**, list pitch **22 px** | `UI.CheckBox`'s own height; the text line measured by GDI+ is 16-17 px, so max(18, line + 2). |
| Group box title band | **18 px** | 1 px offset + the title's GDI+ line height (17). |
| Content below the title | **4 px** (not 8) | Refined from the first gallery pass: the band's bottom already holds the font's internal leading, so 8 px below it put the first row 12 px under the title's letters, against 8 px at the box's bottom: the top looked loose and the box unbalanced. 4 px gives the same 8 px of air under the letters as at the bottom. |
| Section gap | **12 px** | Rows that were 12 px or more apart keep 12 px when a row hidden in the designer stood between them or one of them is a separator line (round 5; before, every such gap stayed, main's loose spacing too). It is not above the gap the grid makes, so a second run keeps it. |
| Caption gap | **2 px** | A label standing above the control it names (a list's title). |
| Check box lead | **16 px** | `UI.CheckBox`: 12 px box + 1 px line + 3 px before the text; the stock check box 18 px. |
| Band gap | **16 px** | Between two label + field bands of one row (where a window has two columns of fields). |

## 2. The tool, and how a rollout slice runs it

**The parts.**
- `tests/UiRefreshTests/GridDump.cs` (opt-in, `OPMS_UI_GRID_DUMP` names the output TSV, `OPMS_UI_GRID_WINDOWS` the windows; default the windows
  of `Data/grid-windows.tsv`): builds each window off screen at the program's font (the harness of the UI-10 text-fit scan, `WindowBuilder`), and
  writes every control: bounds, client area, dock, anchor, AutoSize, CheckAlign, TextAlign, the text on one line measured the way the control
  draws it, a group box's title band, a text box's own height.
- `tools/UiFit/grid_tools.py plan --tree <dump> --out <plan.tsv> --unplaced <tsv>` (the planner): reads the tree with each window's designer and
  code files (`fit_tools.window_files`; controls the code positions are found in the window's `.cs` files: `Location`, `Left`, `Top`, `Bounds`,
  `Size`, `Width`, `Height` set, `SetBounds`, `LayoutManager.Move*`). Per container, children first: it merges controls stacked on the same
  bounds (switched by `Visible` at run time) into one item, cuts the container into rows (and, where no row line exists, into columns; a label
  is never parted from the field it names), pairs each label with its field, clusters the fields of a column by their left edge, and places
  everything by the rules of section 1 (the numbers are the table `GRID` at the top of the file). Containers grow to their content; a group box
  also shrinks to it (equal padding top and bottom); containers that nearly span their parent span it; group boxes side by side, and the i-th
  boxes of columns of boxes, get one top and one height; the window's bottom bar sits 8 px from the bottom with OK/Save/Cancel/Close bottom right.
  **The rules run again on their own result until a pass changes nothing** (at most 6 passes): one pass is not a fixed point, because the cut
  of the laid-out window can find other rows than the cut of the drawn one. The plan is the last pass against the drawn window, so **a plan
  made from the build of an applied plan is empty** (the pilot's re-run: section 4). The plan TSV: designer file, window, control, property
  (Location, Size, ClientSize, AutoSize, CheckAlign, TextAlign), old, new, rule. The `--unplaced` TSV lists what the planner could not place
  (overlapping controls kept as drawn, check boxes with no room to wrap, the windows by hand) and its own check of the planned layout.
  A stale tree (the designer files no longer match the build) is refused per window.
- `grid_tools.py apply --plan <plan.tsv>` (the applier): edits the designer files as bytes (latin-1 round trip), changing only the digits of a
  `Location`/`Size`/`ClientSize` line or one alignment word, or inserting one line before the control's `Name` line with that line's own line
  end: every line end and BOM stay (`tools/Test-NoEolFlips.ps1 -Base main`: 0 flips). The same applier writes the hand plan
  `tests/UiRefreshTests/Data/grid-hand.tsv` (the same columns), so hand edits are data too and re-apply to the same bytes.
- `tests/UiRefreshTests/Data/grid-windows.tsv`: window, slice, mode (`tool`: the planner lays it out; `hand`: listed, its edits are hand rows), note.
- `tests/UiRefreshTests/Data/grid-scope.tsv` (round 2): window, container, controls, keep, note. A window listed here is laid out **in
  parts**: each row is a container laid out whole (its path in the tree) or named controls of a container laid out as one region (`keep`
  = `width`: the part keeps its width and its fields shrink to the room). When a part grows, what stands below it keeps its distance to it
  (moves down), what stands right of it moves right, its container and the window grow by as much, and controls on the window's bottom
  edge stay on it (tall ones grow). Every distance is measured on the window as it was before the pass, so two parts side by side push
  once. This is the "keep the width" mode the rollout needs for windows whose other parts the code lays out (FormApptEdit, FormPayment).
- Round 2 planner rules besides section 1: date pickers (`ODDatePicker`, its box 63 px inside its bounds) and clinic pickers (their own
  label in the first 37 px) are aligned by their box; a `RightToLeft` check box or radio button counts as box-right (the applier sets
  `RightToLeft.No`); columns that hold group boxes are cut as columns before rows (no band of empty space beside a tall box); a row of
  controls hidden in the designer goes to the end of its column; a check box drawn taller than its one line of text is one line tall;
  fields narrowed in one pass widen again when their container grew; notes and their buttons one per row are centred on their column; a
  wide note box keeps its right edge when its column moves right; a wide grid ending a little short of its column reaches it; the bottom
  bar's buttons stand on one line; a control only sized in code (`MoveHeight`, `Width =`) moves with its row; commented-out code is not
  read as positioning; a button that names the box beside it (Lab, Req) stands where a label would.
- `grid_tools.py plan --simulate --tree <a --tree-out file>`: plans the planned trees again without designer checks; the offline idempotence check.
- **Labels whose text the code sets** (slice 1, fix round 8). The tree measures a control's designer text; a window's code may give a label
  another text at run time (FormClinicEdit's and FormCarrierEdit's Canadian address labels, FormScheduleDayEdit's two-line day, FormAdjust's
  percent amount). Each such text is a row of `tests/UiRefreshTests/Data/grid-runtime-texts.tsv` (window, control, text with `\n` for a line
  break, its width on one line at Segoe UI 9, why); `grid_hand.py`'s `Hand.lw` sizes a label for the widest of its texts, and
  `GridRulesTests` sets each text in the laid-out window and fails when it is cut (or when the recorded width is stale). A slice adds the
  rows of its windows: search the window's `.cs` files for `label...Text=` and `check...Text=` with a literal or a formatted value, and
  give a formatted value its widest sample (a five-digit amount, the longest day name). A picture that matters at run time (the day label)
  gets the runtime text in `FitGallery.FillSampleContent`.
- **Layout scripts** (round 3, `tools/UiFit/grid_hand.py` and one script per window in `tools/UiFit/hand/<Window>.py`): for windows the planner
  does not settle (nested panels, modules laid out by their code or by the Chart layout sheet), the script names the window's rows and columns
  and places them with the numbers of `GRID` (the field height measured from the tree, the pitch, the label gap, the padding, the title band):
  `Box.field(label, field, x, w, after=...)`, `Box.checks(...)`, `Box.place(...)`, `h.set(...)`. `grid_hand.py --tree <planned tree> --window
  <Window>` writes the rows into `grid-hand.tsv` (rule `by hand (layout script): ...`, replacing the window's earlier script rows) and prints
  its own check (overlaps by text, controls past their container, labels narrower than their text); `--dry --tree-out <tsv>` draws the result
  with `render.py` before a build. A window can mix the two: its tabs laid out by the planner in keep-width parts (`grid-scope.tsv`; the group
  boxes inside a keep-width part grow to their content, round 3) and the rest by its script, which reads the planner's tree.

**A rollout slice (UI-12-N).**
1. `git switch -c ui/ui12-N main`; add the slice's windows (the next files of `fit-scan-files.tsv` in rank order, about 40 designer files) to
   `grid-windows.tsv` with mode `tool`.
2. Build the filter through the lane (x64), then dump the tree of the slice's windows:
   `set OPMS_UI_GRID_DUMP=<folder>\tree-before.tsv` (and `OPMS_UI_GRID_WINDOWS=<types>` to dump only the slice), Targeted run of `UiRefreshTests`.
3. `python tools/UiFit/grid_tools.py plan --tree <folder>\tree-before.tsv --windows <types> --out <folder>\plan.tsv --unplaced <folder>\unplaced.tsv`,
   then `apply --plan <folder>\plan.tsv`. Commit.
4. Build `OpenPmsDental` and `UiRefreshTests` together (the test bin keeps its own copy of the dll), draw the gallery (`FitGallery` with
   `OPMS_UI_FIT_GALLERY_FONT=program`), and LOOK at every picture. A window the rules place badly: either a rule change in `GRID`/the planner
   (re-plan from main: restore the slice's designer files from `main`, plan from the tree of `main`, apply) or mode `hand` with hand rows.
5. Dump the after build and plan again: the plan must be empty (or only rows of controls of other designers, listed). Add the findings
   `GridRulesTests` reports that the planner also lists, with their reason, to `grid-known.tsv`; fix the rest.
6. Mark the slice's `fit-fixes.tsv` rows that the grid changed (`; superseded by the UI-12 grid`), rewrite the layout snapshot
   (`OPMS_UI_LAYOUT_SNAPSHOT_WRITE=1`), audit the gallery (`UI-12-audit.md` style), Targeted tests.

Since UI-12T the data files of these steps are folders of one file per slice, steps 2-6 are one script, and the batches come from a plan
(next section).

### 2.1 Tooling (UI-12T)

Package UI-12T (the owner, 2026-11-22: "the goal is to speed up the work"): tools and tests only, no window changes.

- **Per-slice data.** `grid-windows`, `grid-hand`, `grid-known`, `grid-runtime-texts` and `layout-snapshot` under
  `tests/UiRefreshTests/Data` are folders of `pilot.tsv`, `1.tsv`, `2.tsv`, ...; every file of a folder has the same comment lines and
  header, and the tools (`slice_data.py`) and tests (`Scan/SliceData.cs`) read the union in slice order. A slice writes only its own
  files: `grid_hand.py` writes the window's slice file, `plan_batches.py --add` adds windows to `grid-windows/<slice>.tsv`, and the
  snapshot writer puts a designer file's rows in the file of its owner (the first slice with a window of the file's class name or a
  hand row in it), `unsliced.tsv` holding the rest. A slice never edits `unsliced.tsv`: it keeps its rows of files a slice now owns,
  and the slice file wins over it. The superseded marks of step 6 are no longer note edits of `fit-fixes.tsv`: a slice lists the rows
  in `fit-fixes-superseded/<slice>.tsv` (designer_file, window, control). `tools/UiFit/split_grid_data.py` makes the folders from the
  single files (a branch made before the split, merged in) or from the folders themselves (it also prunes `unsliced.tsv`).
  It is deterministic: a second run changes nothing, `--check` exits 1 when a run would change a file. The lead reruns it after each
  merge. `SliceDataTests` fails on a window (or snapshot row) in two files, a row outside its window's slice file, a superseded row
  that `fit-fixes.tsv` does not have, or a mark back in its notes.
- **One source for the numbers.** `Data/grid-numbers.tsv` (name, value, why: padding, title gap, label gap, row and list gaps, pitch
  27, list pitch 22, field height, the gutters, the snaps, label cap 0.5, ...) is what `grid_tools.py` builds `GRID` from (and
  `grid_hand.py` uses), with pitch and list pitch checked against their sums; `CodeBase/UI/UiGrid.cs` has the same numbers as
  constants for product code (`UiGrid.PAD`, `UiGrid.PITCH`, ...), and `GridNumbersTests` fails when the two differ. `GridRulesTests`
  takes its padding, dialog gap and label gap from `UiGrid`.
- **Window-family templates** (`tools/UiFit/grid_templates.py`). From the tree alone the planner names a window's family: a **list
  window** (one grid, list or tree view of 35% of the client area or more, its buttons, filters above or beside it), a **report filter**
  (date controls or clinic and provider pickers, small lists, check boxes, a Run/OK button, no big grid) or a **simple edit dialog**
  (label + field rows, check boxes, a bottom bar; group boxes one level deep). A window the general rules leave with unplaced rows, that
  no person has decided on (no layout script, hand rows or scope), is planned again with its template: the drawn overlaps the rules
  would keep as drawn are taken apart first (a label run into its field ends 6 px before it, a label after a tall control starts 6 px
  after it, boxes of one row drawn into each other part, controls of one column drawn 1-8 px into each other stack), and a list
  window's grid then fills the room beside its button column and above its bar. The template's plan is taken when it settles the window
  (a row `template <family>: ...` in the unplaced TSV), otherwise the general plan stays (`the <family> template does not settle it
  either (...)`). `--no-templates` plans with the general rules only. The planner's own check no longer lists the parts of a control the
  window does not lay out (ODDatePicker's box and hidden calendar, ODDateRangePicker's two pickers): 106 of the 165 rows it listed for
  the next windows were these. **No earlier window moves:** the 94 windows of grid-windows re-planned from slice 1's final tree (`G-UI3-ui12-1-src/work/tree-d2.tsv`)
  give 237 rows (pilot) and 461 rows (slice 1), byte-identical to `plan-rerun-pilot-d2.tsv` and `plan-rerun-d2.tsv` (section 6); no
  template is used on them. **Dry run on the next 100 unlaid windows** of `fit-scan-files.tsv` (rank order, the OpenPmsDental and
  OpenPmsDentalGraph windows; the three of CentralManager and the one of DocumentationBuilder in between left out): 70 settle with no
  unplaced row with the planner of slice 1, 85 with the check fix, **88 with the templates** (FormTimeCard as a list window,
  FormGlobalSecurity as an edit dialog, FormRpBrokenAppointments as a report filter). 48 of the 100 are of a family (20 list, 11
  report, 17 edit); 4 of them stay unsettled (FormBilling and FormFinanceCharges reach no fixed point, FormCertifications has lists laid
  over its grid, UserControlSetupWizSchedule's day boxes have no room); the 8 unsettled others are modules and windows with panels laid over one another (ControlAppt, ControlFamily, FormClaimsSend, FormTaskEdit,
  FormAdvertisingPatientList, FormGraphics, FormPayPeriodManager, FormPath) and need scripts or hand rows as before.
- **The slice runner** `tools/UiFit/Run-Slice.ps1 -Slice <n> [-Batch <b> | -Windows <list>] -BuildScript <odbuild.ps1>`: steps 1-6's
  mechanical chain: `add` (the batch into `grid-windows/<n>.tsv`), `before` (build OpenPmsDental and UiRefreshTests through the lane, one
  Targeted UiRefreshTests run that dumps `tree-before.tsv` and draws the before gallery at 100%, one at 150%), `plan` (plan, apply, the
  slice's hand rows), `after` (build again, the test bin's OpenPmsDental.dll checked against the build's by SHA-256, dump
  `tree-after.tsv`, the after gallery at 100% and 150%, the snapshot written on the first run), `replan` (must be empty) and `snapshot`.
  `-Steps` picks steps, `-Print` shows the commands; it commits nothing and shows no window. The before gallery is the branch point's
  build (main), drawn before the plan is applied. Everything lands in `-Work` (default `%TEMP%\ui12-slice-<n>`).
- **The batch plan** `tools/UiFit/plan_batches.py` writes [UI-12-batches.tsv](UI-12-batches.tsv) (batch, kind, rank, designer_file,
  window, controls, group): the designer files no slice owns, in the rank order of `fit-scan-files.tsv` and then by path; a window and
  the user controls its InitializeComponent creates are one group (library controls under `UI/`, or created by more than three windows,
  stand alone); groups of 20 or fewer designer controls go into batches of about 120 windows, larger ones about 50; no file is in two
  batches, so any two batches (consecutive ones too) can run in parallel. Now: 915 designer files in 11 batches (7 small, 4 large). The
  lead reruns it after each merge.
- **Tests.** `python -m unittest discover -s tools/UiFit -p "test_*.py"` (20 tests: the split current and re-runnable, a branch of single
  files merged in, the snapshot pruning, the numbers, the batches, the families and the untangling); in `UiRefreshTests`
  `SliceDataTests` (7) and `GridNumbersTests` (2).

## 4. Pilot results, windows by hand, and the adjustments

**Laid out by the tool (40 windows):** FormPatientEdit, FormProvEdit, the 28 preference pages of UI-10 slice 1, FormPractice,
FormProcCodeEdit, FormInsBenefits, FormScreenEdit, FormBackup, FormStatementOptions, FormEmailInbox, FormApptViewEdit, FormHL7DefEdit,
FormMedLabEdit. The planner's plan from `main`'s tree: 2,934 rows (Location, Size, ClientSize, AutoSize, CheckAlign, TextAlign) in 40
designer files. **The plan from the tree of the applied build has 0 rows** (the re-run, section 2).

**By hand (7 windows, mode `hand` in `grid-windows.tsv`, kept as on `main` but for their hand rows in `grid-hand.tsv`):**

| Window | Why the tool does not place it | Hand rows |
|---|---|---|
| ControlChart | A module laid out by its own code at run time (panels sized and moved in code); the tool grew the module | The procedure buttons B/F, V, M, O/I, D, L and the surface box: a 3 x 3 grid of 26 x 19 px buttons, 2 px apart, centred under a surface box as wide as the grid |
| ControlAccount | A module laid out by its own code at run time; the tool moved its right panel and widened it | none |
| FormProcEdit | Its tooth, surface and arch panels are stacked and switched and placed by its code: the cut loses them, and its rows overlap the code-placed parts | none |
| FormPayment | Its top rows are labels and fields the cut parts (the labels' bounds overlap two rows); the window widened to 1440 px | none: the owner's row is the input style (`UiInputFrame`) |
| FormApptEdit | Its fields column is a 234 px panel between two grids: with the labels as wide as their text and the 6 px gap the panel grows to 336 px and pushes the quick-add list and the grids to a 1590 px window. The rollout needs a "keep the width" mode where fields shrink to the room | The quick-add note: as wide as the list and as tall as its three lines |
| FormClaimEdit | Nested tab pages of group boxes the window sizes in code, buttons stacked at the bottom; the owner's row was the gallery's drawing | none |
| FormInsPlan | The tool's layout is mostly good but grows the window from 686 to 963 px and overlaps the subscriber panel with the benefit grid (placed in code) | The ortho "Wait 30 days..." check box: box at the content edge, text on one line |

**Rules folded into the tool after looking at the pictures** (each repeated over several windows):
- A label is never parted from the field it names (a vertical cut between a label column and a field column made each its own stack and
  lost the row alignment): the cut keeps them together, and a row held together only by such a pair is laid out side by side.
- Columns that a straight line cannot part (FormProcCodeEdit's time bar with the minutes box under it) are parted where every left item is
  left of every right item it shares a height with; the parts keep their x.
- A check box's note follows its text with 6 px (not the next column's x): "Is Prosthesis (crown, bridge, ...)".
- Check boxes in a stack of label + field rows go to the field column only when all of them fit there; otherwise all go to the content edge
  (one edge per group; a check box text wrapped into 3-4 lines in a narrow field column read worse than the label column beside it).
- A check box that the cut parted from its label + field rows still puts its box on the most used field column of its container when
  nothing is in the way (FormProcCodeEdit's "Bypass Global Lock Date").
- Containers that nearly span their parent's content (within 32 px) span it exactly: equal margins left and right (the preference pages'
  group boxes were 20 px from the left and 24 px from the right).
- Group boxes side by side get one height, and columns of group boxes with the same count get one top and one height per row
  (FormStatementOptions' 2 x 2 groups).
- A note that wraps is as tall as its lines (FormPractice's Billing Address note had its first line under the title).
- Label + field columns of one stack that never share a row are one column (the preference groups had their fields at three x values: rows
  drawn with right-aligned fields of different widths).
- A label beside a list or box that starts on its row pairs with it (FormScreenEdit's Race/Ethnicity and Urgency lists join the field column).
- The content of a group box starts 4 px below the title band, not 8 (section 1.1).
- Dialog buttons named OK, Save, Cancel or Close at the bottom of the window go to the bottom right even when they are not anchored there.
- The rules run to a fixed point (section 2): the first pilot pass planned 292 more rows when re-run on its own build.

**What the gallery cannot show:** windows whose Load moves controls (FormInsBenefits puts the benefit grid under its simplified panel at
run time: in the picture the grid sits where the designer has it, over the panel's lower edge). The harness builds windows without Load.

**Round 2** (2026-11-21, the owner's answers and the lead's audit; every window re-planned from the branch point `b58c693c3`):

  FormPreferences grows taller: its page area is 836 px (the tallest page, UserControlApptAppearance; client area 750 x 872, was 696), so
  no page scrolls (`AutoScroll` stays on for a page that one day grows taller).
- The lead's audit, fixed: the Preferences pages in the Preferences style (one field column per page; UserControlApptGeneral's combo boxes

## 5. For the lead

**The pilot's commands** (worktree root; `<G>` = `.git/od-tools/test-results/G-UI3-ui12p-src`, the raw pictures and work files):
```
rem 1. The tree of main (built at the branch point): GridDump, with the before gallery and the style pictures in the same run
set OPMS_UI_GRID_DUMP=<G>\work\tree-before.tsv
set OPMS_UI_FIT_GALLERY=<G>\before
set OPMS_UI_FIT_GALLERY_FONT=program
set OPMS_UI_FIT_GALLERY_WINDOWS=<the 47 types of tests\UiRefreshTests\Data\grid-windows.tsv, comma separated>
set OPMS_UI_FIT_GALLERY_OWNER=<G>\work\owner-rows.tsv
set OPMS_UI_GRID_STYLES=<G>\before
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File <od-tools>\run-tests.ps1 -Root . -Mode Targeted -Platform x64 -RequireBuildOutput -Include UiRefreshTests
rem 2. Plan and apply (and the hand plan)
python tools\UiFit\grid_tools.py plan --tree <G>\work\tree-before.tsv --out <G>\work\plan.tsv --unplaced <G>\work\unplaced.tsv
python tools\UiFit\grid_tools.py apply --plan <G>\work\plan.tsv
python tools\UiFit\grid_tools.py apply --plan tests\UiRefreshTests\Data\grid-hand.tsv
rem 3. Build OpenPmsDental and UiRefreshTests together, then the same run as 1 with <G>\after and tree-after.tsv
rem    (OPMS_UI_LAYOUT_SNAPSHOT_WRITE=1 once, to rewrite Data\layout-snapshot.tsv)
rem 4. The re-run: a plan from the after tree must be empty
python tools\UiFit\grid_tools.py plan --tree <G>\work\tree-after.tsv --out <G>\work\plan-rerun.tsv
rem 5. The page
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\New-UiGallery.ps1 -Before <G>\gal-before -After <G>\gal-after -Name G-UI3 -Out .git\od-tools\test-results\G-UI3-ui12p
```
`<G>\work\assemble.sh` sorts the raw pictures into the page's three sections (1-owner-rows numbered in the review's order with the owner's
note as the clip note, 2-pilot-windows, 3-styles).

**Round 2's commands** (the scripts are in `<G>/work2`): `replan.sh` (restore the pilot designer files from the branch point
`b58c693c3`, plan from `work/tree-before.tsv` with `grid-windows.tsv` and `grid-scope.tsv`, apply, apply `grid-hand.tsv`, set
FormPreferences' page area), `gal.sh <folder> <result> [build]` (build `OpenPmsDental` and `UiRefreshTests` together and check that the two
copies of `OpenPmsDental.dll` have one sha256, then the Targeted `UiRefreshTests` run with the gallery, the style pictures and the tree
dump; `SNAP=1` rewrites the layout snapshot), `assemble.sh` (the page's three sections from `before` and `r2f`), then
`tools/New-UiGallery.ps1 -Before <G>/gal-before -After <G>/gal-r2f -Name G-UI3 -Out .git/od-tools/test-results/G-UI3-ui12p`. The re-run
check: `grid_tools.py plan --tree <G>/work2/tree-r2f.tsv --out plan-rerun.tsv` plans nothing (with the round's last hand rows applied).
`sim.sh` plans in simulation (`--simulate --tree-out`) and `render.py` draws a planned tree as boxes: a look at a rule change in seconds,
before a build.

**Round 3's commands** (the scripts are in `<G>/work3`): `replan3.sh` (restore the pilot designer files from the branch point, plan from
`work/tree-before.tsv` with `grid-windows.tsv` and `grid-scope.tsv` into `work3/tree-planned.tsv`, run the five layout scripts on that tree
(`grid_hand.py --tree work3/tree-planned.tsv --window <W>`: FormInsPlan, ControlChart, ControlAccount, FormProcEdit, FormClaimEdit; they
rewrite their rows in `grid-hand.tsv`), apply the plan, apply `grid-hand.tsv`, set FormPreferences' page area); `work2/gal.sh r3i
ui12p-r3i build` with `SNAP=1` (build, the gallery, the style pictures, the tree dump, the layout snapshot); `work3/assemble.sh` (the
page's sections from `before` and `r3i`); `tools/New-UiGallery.ps1 -Before <G>/gal-before -After <G>/gal-r3i -Name G-UI3 -Out
.git/od-tools/test-results/G-UI3-ui12p`. The offline re-run check: `work3/check.sh` (plan main's tree in simulation, apply the hand rows to
the planned tree with `treehand.py`, plan again: in simulation it lists only the responsible party row's 1 px rounding, which the
real re-run on the build's tree does not plan).

**Gallery** G-UI3-ui12p (redrawn from round 3, `G-UI3-ui12p.zip`): 91 pictures, `1-owner-rows` (the 38 rows, numbered), `2-pilot-windows`
(47), `3-styles` (FormPayment's inputs, the procedure buttons and the page bar at 100% and 150%). The before pictures are main's, drawn in
round 1. The harness builds windows without Load: FormInsBenefits' benefit grid is drawn where the designer has it (Load moves it under
the simplified panel); FormProcEdit's tooth, surface and area fields are hidden in the designer (FillControls shows the treatment area's
ones), so the picture shows their rows empty; at 150% the harness scales bounds but not the native up-down boxes' and date pickers' font
heights (the boxes are lower than the text boxes beside them in the picture only).

**Tests** (`ui12p-r3-final.txt`, the whole filter built x64 first: 0 errors): `UiRefreshTests` 262 passed (16 opt-in skipped;
`GridRulesTests` in every grid window with the known findings of `grid-known.tsv`), `WindowChecksTests` 30 passed (44 opt-in skipped),
`UnitTests` 1728 passed (3 skipped), `PrintGuardTests` 19 passed (1 skipped), `NoHqTests` 127 passed. `tools/Test-NoEolFlips.ps1 -Base
main`: 0 flips. The audit (`UI-12-audit.md`, round 3, pass 3) finds nothing beyond the known rows and the harness's FormClaimEdit bottom bar.

**Open after round 3** (kept as on main, with their reasons in the layout scripts' headers and `grid-known.tsv`; for the rollout slices):
- ControlChart's Missing, Movements, Show and Ortho pages (dense pages in the layout sheet's fixed tab size) and ControlAccount's hidden
  insurance and discount summary groups and its Auto Ortho page.

**Round 4's commands** (the fix round, branch `ui/ui12p-2`; the scripts are in `<G>/work4`): `replan4.sh` (round 3's `replan3.sh` with
the round 4 rules: restore the pilot designer files from the branch point, plan from `work/tree-before.tsv`, run the five layout scripts on
the planned tree, apply the plan, apply `grid-hand.tsv`, set FormPreferences' page area; a second run after the round's last commit
reproduces the designer files byte for byte); `sim4.sh <windows>` (plan in simulation, apply the hand rows with `treehand.py`, plan again,
draw the windows with `render.py`); `hand.py <spec.tsv>` (writes hand rows: window, control, property, new value, rule; the old value from
the simulated tree); `work2/gal.sh r4e ui12p-r4e` after the whole filter's build (`SNAP=1` once, in r4c, for the layout snapshot);
`assemble4e.sh`; `tools/New-UiGallery.ps1 -Before <G>/gal-before -After <G>/gal-r4e -Name G-UI3 -Out .git/od-tools/test-results/G-UI3-ui12p`.
The applier takes one new hand-row property, `AddBefore` (moves a control's `Controls.Add` line before a sibling's, so it draws in front:
FormScreenEdit's age arrows).

**The re-run** (`grid_tools.py plan --tree <G>/work2/tree-r4e.tsv`): 123 rows, all in the 12 windows whose round 4 hand rows override a
choice of the planner (FormInsBenefits 25, UserControlEnterpriseGeneral 16, FormEmailInbox 12, FormPatientEdit, FormPayment, FormScreenEdit
and FormStatementOptions 11 each, FormClaimEdit 9, FormApptViewEdit 8, FormMedLabEdit 5, UserControlMainWindow 2, FormProvEdit and
UserControlAccountGeneral 1): the plan would move them back to the planner's own layout. A slice applies the plan first and the hand rows
last, so the hand rows win; the windows that need them more than once in the rollout are where the planner should learn the rule
(equal group heights in a row, notes beside their buttons, a group's hidden last row, keep-the-bottom for tab controls).

**Gallery** G-UI3-ui12p (redrawn from round 4, `G-UI3-ui12p.zip`): 93 pictures: the 38 owner rows, the 47 windows, the style pictures at
100% and 150% (FormPayment's inputs, FormPayment with its splits hidden, the procedure buttons, the page bar). The harness now lays a
window out again after creating its handles, at its designer size (FormClaimEdit's bottom bar and right margin, FormEmailInbox drawn at
its designer size, not maximized). At 150% the date pickers' and up-down boxes' text looks larger than the labels' in the picture: the
harness scales their fonts as Windows does at 144 dpi but leaves the labels at their zoom font (the program's windows scale both).

**Tests** (`ui12p-r4-final.txt`, the whole filter built x64 first: 0 errors): `UiRefreshTests` 262 passed (16 opt-in skipped),
`WindowChecksTests` 30 passed (44 opt-in skipped), `UnitTests` 1728 passed (3 skipped), `PrintGuardTests` 19 passed (1 skipped),
`NoHqTests` 127 passed. `tools/Test-NoEolFlips.ps1 -Base main`: 0 flips. The audit's round 4 passes are in `UI-12-audit.md`.

**Owner questions from round 4** (the lead's decisions are welcome; the round went with the first answer each time):
1. Finding 8 asked for the inputs at `Scale(23)` (34 px at 150%); the date picker and the up-down box cannot be sized (Windows locks them
   to their font's height, 31 px at 150%), so round 4 sizes the program's combo boxes and the text boxes sized by their bounds to the
   text box's height at the scaled font instead (31 px). One height per row either way; this way needs no fight with Windows.
2. FormEmailInbox's source lists are 50 px tall (three items) so the three filter groups share the Search group's height; the alternative
   is all three at the Sent Message Sources group's 168 px with empty space in the other two.

### Slice 1, continuation (the lead's answers of 2026-11-22)

On `ui/ui12-1-1` from its first run (`a8239e017`). The lead's two answers, what the first run left open, and the fix rounds of the audit's
passes 4 and 5.

**The clinic picker's label area (answer 1).** `ComboBoxClinicPicker.WidthLabelArea` (new, read only): the label's text as a WinForms Label
measures it at the control's font + the grid's 6 px label gap, so the label stands right-aligned 6 px before the box like the labels of the
rows around it and "Clinics" is never cut. At Segoe UI 9: "Clinic" 43 px, "Clinics" 48 (main: a fixed 37, which cut "Clinics" at its
left and drew "Clinic" 2 px from the box). Scaled, it is the 96 dpi width scaled as LayoutManager scales the bounds around it (the text
measured at the font less the scale; a text measured at 150% is a few pixels narrower than 1.5 times its 100% width, which put the box
3 px left of its column), never narrower than the text at the control's font and the scaled gap; cached per text, font and scale. The
label is drawn on one line, unclipped, ending 6 px before the box. The planner places a picker's box on its column with the new area
(`CLINIC_LABEL_W`: "Clinic" 43, "Clinics" 48; a picker set to `IsMultiSelect` in its designer or its window's code reads "Clinics",
`multi_pickers_in_code`: FormRpTreatmentFinder sets it in Load), the layout scripts with `Hand.clinic_w`; `GridDump` writes a picker's label
width (`text_width`), and a tree without it (a build before this change, main's 37 px control) keeps the picker's drawn box width (`w0`).
`GridRulesTests` and the list-control state pictures read `WidthLabelArea`. Every window of `grid-windows.tsv` with a picker lines up
(`GridRulesTests` passes): the slice's pickers by the re-plan (x less 6, or 11 for "Clinics", the width more by as much), the pilot's by
their rows: FormPatientEdit, FormApptEdit and FormPayment by the planner's rows (`work/plan-pilot-picker-apply.tsv`), FormClaimEdit and
FormProcEdit by their scripts' rows; FormApptViewEdit's hand row keeps the picker 6 px after the mirrored-appointments check box's text (its
box 6 px narrower). Windows outside the grid with a picker show its box 6 px (11 for "Clinics") further right than main until their slice.

**Pickers outside the grid (the lead's condition, continuation 2).** The lead allowed the 6-11 px until their slice on condition that the
picker's outer bounds do not change, nothing overlaps or runs past a neighbour, and the selected text still fits. In the code
(`ComboBoxClinicPicker.cs`): `WidthLabelArea` is read only and used inside the control alone: the paint draws the box from it to the
control's own right edge (`rectangleCombo.X=WidthLabelArea`, the box to `Width`), the mouse tests and the drop-down's width
(`Width-WidthLabelArea`) follow it; nothing sets the control's `Location`, `Size` or `Bounds` from it, so the bounds are the designer's and
LayoutManager's as on main: the box gets narrower, its right edge stays, and no neighbour can be overlapped or run past that was not before
(`GridRulesTests` checks the 22 pickers of the grid windows; the bounds of the other 57 are unchanged, so the overlap and edge checks of the
fit scan see what they saw on main). The label is never cut (its area is its measured text + 6 px). The selected text is drawn in the box
less its chevron and is clipped at the box as on main; one clinic shows its abbreviation, several "Multiple" when they do not fit. The scan of
every picker with a label (`work/pickerscan.py`, `work/pickerscan-b6.txt`: 79 pickers, 22 in grid windows): the narrowest box outside the
grid is FormApptSearchAdvanced's, 124 px (main 130), then FormRpClaimNotSent's "Clinics", 128 (main 139), FormDunningEdit's 131 (137);
every box outside the grid keeps at least 108 px for its text (its width less the chevron), room for "Multiple" and a clinic
abbreviation of about 16 characters (main: about 17), so the
outer bounds did not change anywhere and main's box position is not needed.

**One margin (answer 2).** The report windows on the grid's 8 px: FormRpEraAutoProcessed (a new script: main's 12 px frame; the filter
row's left part 4 px left, the rest 4 px right, the three grids from edge to edge) and FormRpUnearnedIncome's four pages (their content from
18 px to the page's 8 px edge, each OK 8 px from the page's corner). The slice's other report windows were on 8 already
(FormRpDPPOvercharged, FormRpProcOverpaid, FormRpAging, FormRpCustomAging by the first run's scripts).

**ControlTreat** (mode hand, `tools/UiFit/hand/ControlTreat.py`). What its code does, checked in `ControlTreat.cs`: it moves and sizes none
of its controls (the print preview's tooth chart wrapper is its own); it switches by `Visible` the insurance summaries against the discount
summary (`FillSummary`), Plan Appt, Refresh with the Estimates as of date and caption, Ins Rem and some of the Show page's check boxes;
LayoutManager keeps the anchored Procedures grid (top, bottom, left) and the note (bottom, left) on the module's edges. The script: the top
row on one top and one height (155: the Show page's seven check boxes at the grid's 18 px rows), 4 px between its parts (main: 3, 6, 2),
New TP and Save TP from the top, Refresh and Plan Appt ending on the row's bottom, the Pre Authorizations grid to the right column's edge;
the Procedures grid 4 px under the row and 4 px from the right column; the right column's captions 2 px over their list and date, Set
Priority's list ending 4 px over the summaries, the summaries stacked from the Procedures grid's bottom 4 px apart (the discount summary on
the family summary's place: one or the other shows), Ins Rem on the column's edge; the Sort page's note at main's 159 px (its fit fix).
`grid-known.tsv`: the summaries' overlap is gone (removed); the discount summary's own boxes stay (DashIndividualDiscount, another designer).
Its 8 `fit-fixes.tsv` rows are marked superseded.

**The rounding rows.** FormReportSetup's Report Server page and FormCloneAdd: the planner's second run on its own output rounded five fields
1-2 px (its first run starts the field widths from the drawn ones, `w0`), and FormReportSetup's Misc Settings check box widened to its
grown page: pinned in `grid-hand.tsv` at the planner's fixed point (`by hand: the planner's fixed point, pinned ...`; a simulated third run
plans nothing). FormReportSetup's two stock combo boxes planned 23 px on every run (Windows makes them 21 whatever their designer says): the
planner writes no Size row whose value the designer already has.

**The 150% pictures.** `WindowBuilder.LayOutAt` gives the client panel its whole scaled size when Windows keeps the window within its
largest track size (1170 px on the capture machine), so FormCarrierEdit (1492 px), FormClearinghouseEdit, FormEhrLabResultEdit2014 and the
other tall windows are drawn whole (FormEhrLabResultEdit2014's Add Note is no longer drawn over its groups). The before pictures come from a
build of `main` at the branch point (the product's projects of `05f0cbca7` with this branch's harness, in this worktree through the lane,
then the branch restored: `work/` commands below). The harness scales the clinic picker's font as Windows does at 150% (as it does for the
date pickers and up-down boxes), since its label area follows its text; the other labels keep the harness's zoom font.

**The gallery as the windows run** (`FitGallery.FillSampleContent`): FormReportSetup's Display Settings page shows UserControlReportSetup in
its display mode (its instructions; the user group row is the Security Permissions page's), FormRpTreatmentFinder's picker as Load makes it
(multi select).

shows under Primary Provider, Clone at the bottom right; the planner had put Clone between them), FormMedical (new: the top boxes' captions
2 px over them, the Service Notes and Medical History boxes to the page's content edge, Service Notes down to Medical Summary's bottom),

ControlTreat's 18 px check box rows.

**Fix round 7 (the audit's pass 5).** ControlTreat's picture drew the discount plan summary over the family insurance summary: FillSummary
shows one or the other (the discount summary for a patient with a discount plan, on the family summary's place), the harness built both.
`FitGallery.FillSampleContent` hides the discount summary, so the picture shows the module for a patient without a discount plan (pictures
only: the tree dump and the snapshot do not use it). The before picture, drawn from main's build before this change, still shows both.

**The tools.** The two committed `tools/UiFit/__pycache__/*.pyc` removed; `tools/.gitignore` ignores `__pycache__/` and `*.pyc`.

**What the code positions or sizes** (checked in each `.cs`): FormTaskEdit's `LoadTask` sets the window's width to the split container's
right edge + 25 (`Width=splitContainer.Right+25`): every control stands within that edge (the right column, Copy/Add, TaskNum and Save end on
it), so the code's width still holds the window; the designer's client width is that edge + 8 (1002), as the code makes it at run time
(main's designer said 1185, the code narrowed it to 1018 outer). Its `Load` scales the splitter distance; the scripts keep the designer's
distance (147). The other four set only `Visible` and texts: FormClaimPayBatch hides the instructions, the filters and the attach buttons
by its mode; FormAdvertisingPatientList switches its two panels; FormPayConnectSetup hides the clinic note and Clinic without clinics and
renames the settings group (a shorter text); the wizard page shows a day's group with its box and names the provider (two rows in
`grid-runtime-texts.tsv`, the longer 562 px: the provider line is 568 px wide).

**The harness and the wizard page.** `SetupWizControl` (the wizard pages' base class) has a designer with a title and a description label but
no constructor, so the program never makes those labels; the harness ran every class's `InitializeComponent` and drew them over the page
(they covered the page's provider line, its Next Prov note and the Friday to Sunday boxes in main's picture). `WindowBuilder.CreateBare` now
runs a base class's `InitializeComponent` only when one of that class's constructors calls it (`ConstructorCalls`, an IL scan;
`BuilderAndScanTests.CreateBare_RunsABaseDesignerOnlyWhenItsConstructorDoes`). The only such base in the program is `SetupWizControl`
(every other designer base, FormODBase's windows', calls its own); the pictures and trees of the other wizard pages change the same way
(none is a grid window, and no data file names their base labels). `grid_hand.py` takes a window outside the `OpenPmsDental` namespace by
its full name. The gallery (`FitGallery.FillSampleContent`) draws FormTaskEdit's default state (the reminder group, a weekly reminder) and
the wizard page with every day checked and a provider line.

**The re-plan counts** (the lead's decision of 2026-11-23): slice 1 = 461 rows (`G-UI3-ui12-1-src/work/plan-rerun-d2.tsv`, fix round 9's
final count), pilot = 243 rows (237 in slice 1; FormClaimEdit 7 fewer, FormPractice 13 more, as explained above). From this continuation's
final build (`work/tree-a7.tsv`): slice 1's 47 windows plan 461 rows and the pilot's 47 windows 243 rows, both byte-identical to the `a6`
re-runs (`work/plan-rerun-slice1-a7.tsv`, `work/plan-rerun-pilot-a7.tsv`); the slice's 51 windows plan the same 158 rows as on `a6`, none
in the five hand windows (`work/plan-rerun-a7.tsv`). No planner rule was added (`grid_tools.py` unchanged).

**RendererTests.MenuStripAndToolStrip_DrawInTheTokens** (the lead's item 4). The cause: the disconnected session reports a screen of 0 bits
per pixel (`Screen.PrimaryScreen.BitsPerPixel`; connected, 32). Windows Forms then counts the display as low resolution
(`DisplayInformation.LowResolution`, 8 bits or less), and `ToolStripProfessionalRenderer` hands the strips' drawing to its low-resolution
renderer, which paints them in system colors: the menu bar in `SystemColors.Control` (240, 240, 240), not our renderer's Surface (checked
with a plain renderer in the same session: `LowResolution` true, `HighContrast` false). It is the session, not the renderer: the test is
now inconclusive when the screen reports 8 bits per pixel or less, with a message naming it, as the WPF drawing tests skip; in a session with
a real color depth it checks the same pixels as before.

**Rows.** `grid-hand.tsv`: 429 rows of the five scripts. `grid-known.tsv`: unchanged (GridRulesTests finds nothing in the five). `fit-fixes.tsv`: 33 rows of the five windows marked
`; superseded by the UI-12 grid`. The layout snapshot rewritten from `a7`.

974 x 676, FormAdvertisingPatientList 1230 x 707, FormPayConnectSetup 776 x 708, UserControlSetupWizSchedule 930 x 530: the same.

Branch `ui/ui12-2-2` from `ui/ui12-2-1` (`d2263b596`), same worktree. Each window is laid out again from the branch point by
`work/refit.sh <window>` (its designer back to `647febb65`, its planner rows of `work/plan.tsv` for a tool window, its layout script on
`work/tree-planned.tsv` or `work/tree-m1.tsv`, then its rows of `grid-hand.tsv`); `work/replan.sh` and `work/replan-cont.sh` give the same
designers (`work/scripts.txt` gains FormRpInsAging).

| # | Window | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | FormClaimsSend | The history panel's row from the panel's top: the date range's boxes 8 px under the splitter (its top 6: the range draws its boxes 2 px into its bounds), Type and its box on the boxes' top and height, the history toolbar's gray frame centred on the row (it stays 27 px: the 25 px toolbar and its border; 2 px above and below the boxes) and on the grids' right edge; the history grid 8 px under the frame, to the right content edge, ending 8 px above the window bottom. `FormClaimsSend.cs` (`panelSplitter_MouseMove`) sets the panel's top to the splitter's bottom **and** its height to `ClientSize.Height - Top - 1`, so the panel keeps ending 1 px above the window bottom and the 8 px margins are inside it (the grid is anchored to the panel's bottom). |
| 2 | FormEtrans835s, FormTrackNext, FormUnsched, FormRpProcedures, FormRpClaimNotSent, FormCareCreditTransactions | The date range 2 px over its row wherever it shares one with fields (its boxes on the row's top with Carrier, Clinics, Providers, Status); FormEtrans835s' Amount Range group 9 px under the range's boxes with its Min and Max on the Control ID and Clinics row (the grid 4 px lower). Scripts for the first four, hand rows for the two planner windows without a script. FormRpHiddenPaySplits' range has a row of its own (unchanged). |
| 3 | FormFeeScheds | The right column on one left edge (380, the column of Type, Up, Down and Set Order): Sort, Add, Clean Up Allowed, Hide Unused, each note under its button, left-aligned, to the right content edge and as tall as its lines (one, two, two); 4 px between every item from Set Order down. The audit line's "the centred notes are main's" was wrong: main had them left-aligned at 383. |
| 4 | FormLetterMergeEdit | The three lists and their captions one width (170) and 8 px apart from the content edge (8, 186, 364); the hint box on the Referred From list's left edge. |
| 5 | FormRpInsAging | A layout script (tool mode, on the planner's tree): the left column at the content edge (8); the three lists one width (161), one top (their captions at 8, the All boxes at 29, the lists at 53), 8 px apart, the last ending on the right content edge (774); the future transactions note under the Providers list. |
| 6 | FormProcGroup | The Notes and Signature 2 boxes end on the right content edge with Save (982); the + / Remove column right-aligned with Save (906); the Procedures grid from 8 to 8 px before the column. `FormProcGroup.cs` positions only `butInvalidate`. |

Hand rows (FormConfirmList, FormScreenGroupEdit) and FormTimeCard's script. |

The insurance summaries (`DashFamilyInsurance`, `DashIndividualInsurance`) make their font in `OnPaint` from the zoom only and leave the dpi to the Graphics Windows paints them with; at a scale other than 1 the gallery paints them through `OnPaint` into a picture of 96 x scale dpi (`DesignSheetWindows.PaintDpiScale`), so their text has the size a 150% screen gives it. Pictures only; 100% unchanged. |

FormClaimEdit needed no state: its default picture shows the General tab with the missing teeth note (two lines, whole); the audit line that said otherwise was wrong and is corrected. |

Branch `ui/ui12-4-2` from `ui/ui12-4-1` (`d7f6d3662`), same worktree. Each window of a finding is laid out again from the branch point
by `work/refit.sh <window>` (its designer back to `33d9fce1f`, its rows of `work/plan.tsv`, its layout script on `work/tree-planned.tsv`,
then its rows of `grid-hand/4.tsv`; FormReconcileProblem's removed button removed again). 26 new layout scripts; 66 of the slice's 104
windows now have one.

| # | Window | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | FormReplicationSetup | Add (Bottom\|Right) on the grid's bottom edge 6 px after it; the Set Ranges and Test notes wrap to 6 px before the Replication Failure group, which stands beside them on the band's top (main's place; the notes ran under it). `FormReplicationSetup_Load` no longer sets the literal 901 x 640 (a client area 18 px narrower and 37 px shorter than the designer's): without random primary keys it keeps the designer's width, takes the hidden band's height off the window (`groupBox1.Top-butSetRanges.Top`) and, when the failure group is hidden, grows the grid back to 8 px (`UiGrid.PAD`) over the Force Sync group (with the failure group shown it stands 8 px under the grid). The section's claim that no size conflicts with the layout is corrected above. |
| 2 | FormCarriers, FormClaimPayEditOld, FormEhrPatListResults, FormRecallListUndo | The tree records LayoutManager's reset anchor (Top\|Left, `LayoutManagerForms.SetNoAnchorDock`), so the planner's bottom-anchor rules never fired and moved bottom-anchored side controls to the top of their column. Scripts put them back on their anchored edge: Add and Combine at the bottom of the column (Combine on the grid's bottom edge), Delete and its note (the note ending on the grid's bottom), Print on the grid's bottom, Patients Affected and its count on the OK row. **The check:** `grid_tools.py anchors --tree <tree> [--windows ...]` reads each control's anchor from its designer (`Designer.anchor`) and lists a control anchored to the bottom only whose middle stands in the upper half of a container that resizes, unless it sits on the stretching bottom edge of a Top\|Bottom sibling (exit 1); the layout scripts' own check reports the same. `.git/od-tools/test-results/G-UI3-ui12-3-src`.

**The windows** (`grid-windows/3.tsv`, 37 windows):
- **Laid out by the planner (mode tool, 10):** FormInsPlans, UserControlSecurityTree, FormSecurity (the planner places the hosted user
  control; its own controls are a layout script's, below), and with a layout script on the planner's tree FormInsPlansOverrides,
  FormDepositEdit, FormOperatoryEdit, FormMedPat, FormDisplayFieldOrthoEdit, FormTsiHistory, FormImagingSetup (table below); the fix
  round adds one to FormInsPlans (Get All and Show Hidden 8 px after Order By, as in its twin).
- **Laid out by their own layout script (mode hand, 27):** FormPath, FormBillingDefaults, FormBillingOptions, FormUserQuery,
  FormEmailAddressEdit, FormUserEdit, FormPayPeriodManager, FormCertifications, FormAdjMulti, FormChooseDatabase, FormClockEventEdit,
  FormCreditCardEdit, FormDatabaseMaintenance, FormDatabaseMaintenancePat, FormEClinicalWorks, FormEhrSettings, FormPayConnect2,
  FormProcButtonEdit, FormRpProdGoal, FormMaintItemEdit, FormOrthoChart, FormEtransEdit, FormEhrLabPanelEdit, FormEhrPatientSmoking,
  FormApptSearchAdvanced, FormInfobutton, FormEtrans835ClaimPay. The planner placed each badly (FormBillingDefaults reached no fixed point
  and spread to 4097 px; FormPath to 1416 px and FormUserQuery to 1551 px by making long check boxes one line; overlapping rows kept as
  drawn; captions and field columns at several x), so each script lays the window out from main's arrangement on the grid's numbers.
- **UserControlSecurityUserGroup** (the Security window's content) is not in `grid-windows`: it adds controls in code, which LayoutManager
  allows only in its host, so the harness cannot build it alone (as `PictureMapTests` already lists). Its layout script runs on its
  host's tree: `grid_hand.py --window FormSecurity --part userControlSecurityUserGroup --part-class UserControlSecurityUserGroup` (new
  option: the tree from the part's node down, the part's designer, the rows written under the host window in its slice file with the
  part's designer file, so the snapshot owner rule gives the file to slice 3). GridRulesTests checks it inside FormSecurity.

**Skipped** (not in `grid-windows`; their designers stay as on `main`), each as slices 1 and 2 decided:

| Designer file (rank) | Why |
|---|---|
| CentralManager/FormCentralManager (19), FormCentralPatientSearch (28), FormCentralSecurity (100), FormCentralUserEdit | Plain forms of the Central Manager exe (no LayoutManager, no FormODBase, no input frame): the plain exes' slice. |
| Main Modules/ControlAppt (94) | The appointment module: the appointment book is an owner-drawn surface and the module lays its panels out at run time. |
| OpenPmsDentalGraph/Base/GraphQuantityOverTime (96) with Concrete/GraphQuantityOverTimeFilter (97), Base/GroupingOptionsCtrl, Base/Legend | The dashboard's owner-drawn chart surface and the controls of its cell. |
| OpenPmsDentalGraph/Dashboard/FormPrintImage (98), FormPrintSettings (99) | The dashboard's print preview and print settings: plain Forms without LayoutManager, a printout path. |
| FormClaimFormEdit (106) | The claim form editor: an owner-drawn claim form surface. |

**The layout scripts on the planner's tree** (tool windows): FormDepositEdit (the right column's boxes and the Show group to the right
content edge with Send QB, the group's content from its padding with two even list columns), FormOperatoryEdit (each note 6 px after
the check box or box it explains, on its row; the window 618 px, not the planner's 665), FormMedPat (the Edit note centred on Edit, Remove
at the padding with its note after it), FormDisplayFieldOrthoEdit (the notes on their rows, Up and Down on the Description row),

FormInsPlansOverrides (the Overrides group 8 px after the grid to the right edge, Select All on the grid's edge).

**The scripts of mode hand** (each script's header says what and why): one field column per window wherever the rules allow, the inner
groups' labels keeping their padding (the window's column at 2 x 8 + the widest label + 6); captions 2 px over their boxes; notes as tall
as their measured lines (`Hand.note_h`, new: the measured word wrap, no empty line; `Hand.captioned`, new: a caption over its box with
buttons after it); lists one width and one top; bottom bars 8 px from the edges. Choices worth naming: FormBillingOptions' Filter group in
the Preferences style (mostly check boxes) and the window 110 px wider for it; FormPayPeriodManager's Start Date a captioned box (a label
column would stand 32 px from the Semi-Monthly column); FormEClinicalWorks 78 px wider so the notes after the boxes stay one line;

wider); FormEhrPatientSmoking 64 px wider for its grids' MinimumSize (559 px); FormCertifications' Cert Complete view lists and its
Sort By Date Completed on the bounds of the Category view's (FormCertifications.cs moves them there and switches them by Visible).

### Slice 3, fix round (the lead's review of 2026-11-23, 12 findings)

Branch `ui/ui12-3-2` from `ui/ui12-3-1` (`34f1acbcd`), same worktree. Each window is laid out again from the branch point by
`work/refit3.py <window>` (its designer back to `33d9fce1f`, its planner rows for a tool window, its layout script, then its rows of
`grid-hand/3.tsv`). `grid_tools.py apply` gains two hand-row properties (the planner is unchanged): `BackColor` to an `r,g,b` colour and
`Visible`.

| # | Window | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | FormUserQuery | Real: `FormQuery_Load` sets the upper split panel to `Scale(140)`, and the button panel (147 px) cut the Print Orientation choices by 7 px. Print Orientation's caption now stands on the row of Portrait and Landscape (middle-aligned, the choices 6 px after it): the panel is 128 px from 1 px under the frame, so it ends at 129 (the script asserts it fits 140 less the padding). The "code positions" bullet of "Slice 3 (batch 1)" now names the SplitterDistance line. |
| 2 | The gallery | `FitGallery.FillSampleState` draws FormDatabaseMaintenance `hidden`, `old`, `tools`; FormDatabaseMaintenancePat `hidden`, `old`; FormPath `database`, `removed`, `sftp`; FormUserEdit `clinics`, `alertsubs`; FormSecurity `usergroups` (the hosted user control's User Groups page); FormCertifications `certcomplete` (as `radioCertification_Click` shows it). All 12 at 100%; FormUserEdit's two and FormSecurity's also at 150%. |
| 3 | FormCertifications | The Cert Complete view's five controls `Visible = false` in the designer (hand rows), as Load hides them: the default picture shows the Category view; the other view is the `certcomplete` state. No change in behaviour. |
| 4 | FormUserQuery | Every child of the two split panels 1 px inside them (`FRAME` in the script): the Format group ends 1 px before the right frame line; the query box, the Title row and the results grid start 1 px in; the button panel 1 px under the top line. Measured in `a8`: the frame lines at x 8 and 1267 run unbroken from top to bottom except for the splitter band. Redrawn at 100% and 150%. |

control in any slice's windows (pilot, 1, 2, 4, 5: 0 each, on the final tree).

**GridDump writes the anchor LayoutManager stores.** `GridDump.GetStoredAnchors` reads each control's `Control96Info.Anchor` from the
window's LayoutManager (LayoutManager takes every designer anchor over and leaves the control at Top\|Left, `SetNoAnchorDock`), and the
tree's `anchor` column is that one (the control's own where LayoutManager does not track it). The planner's rules that read anchors (the
bottom bar's bottom-anchored items, Top\|Bottom containers keeping their height, right-anchored items) now see the real ones. This slice's
own plan is made from such a tree.

**Planner and the earlier windows.** Re-planned from this slice's final tree (`work/tree-a4.tsv`, every window of `grid-windows`) against
the counts of slice 4's fix round (`G-UI3-ui12-4-src/work/plan-rerun-<slice>-f2.tsv`, `replan-f2.tsv`):

  FormRpTreatmentFinder, FormArManager, FormClaimPayEdit, FormRxDefEdit, FormCloneAdd, FormPaySplitEdit, FormRpCustomAging,
  FormFamilyBalancer; FormRpDPPOvercharged's and FormRpProcOverpaid's Print no longer moved; FormASAP's rows follow its Print List.
- **slice 2: 203 rows (was 179).** FormConfirmList, FormProgramLinkEdit, FormProcGroup, FormRpInsAging, FormTrackNext, FormRpClaimNotSent,
  FormDunningSetup, the two Arizona reports (their buttons now at the bottom); three merge windows' Merge no longer moved.
- **slice 4: 316 rows (was 240).** The same rule in 40 windows (FormAccountPick, FormCarriers, FormClaimAttachPasteDXC, FormSupplyOrders,
  FormTimeCardManage, FormRpInsPendingSupplementals, the reconcile windows, ...).
- **Every row the stored anchors add or change is pinned** where no hand row covered it: 147 hand rows (pilot 8, slice 1 35, slice 2 21,
  slice 4 83) with rule `by hand (slice 5, stored anchors): pinned at the reviewed layout; ...` keep the designer's value, so the slice
  tools (plan, then hand rows) give the reviewed layouts and `grid_tools.py apply` of every hand file changes no designer. The windows
